I’m just hesitant to say “most days” because I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I do feel kind of off or low at times, but I’m not sure I can say it’s most days. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: So, to clarify, would you say that more often than not, you feel a bit down or low in your mood, even if it's not all day or very strong?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I can honestly say more often than not. Maybe I have moments where I feel low, but it’s not like that most of the time. I just feel kind of not myself, if that makes sense. I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity that you used to really enjoy, even just a little, that you don’t enjoy anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess watching my shows after work, and just sitting with my husband. That used to feel comforting, like a nice part of the day. Now I’ll do it and I know I should feel relaxed or happy or something, but it’s kind of… blank. Like the warm part of me isn’t really there. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific activity that you used to enjoy, even just a little, where you now feel absolutely no interest or pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think it’s that. Sitting with my husband after work, or putting on one of my shows. I used to get at least a little comfort from that, like it felt nice and normal. Now I do it and I know I’m supposed to feel something good, but honestly it’s just… nothing. Like the warm part of me shut off. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, even if not severe, your mood has been low or down on most days over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. I mean, I’ve had times where I feel low or just kind of off, but I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s more like… not feeling like myself, maybe. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like sitting with your husband or watching your favorite shows, even just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s honestly kind of what made me come in. I’ll sit with my husband or put on one of my shows after work, and I know I should feel comforted or even just a little happy, but there’s just… nothing. Like the warm part of me shut off. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, not just the ones we’ve talked about, like sitting with your husband or watching your shows?\n\nPatient: I think… mostly, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s not just the shows or sitting with my husband. It’s like the part of me that would feel comfort or a little happiness just isn’t there right now. I’ll try to do normal things after work, and I know I’m supposed to feel something good, but it’s just kind of blank. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in almost all activities, not just the ones you mentioned like sitting with your husband or watching shows?\n\nPatient: I guess… almost all might be fair, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just refusing to do things, because I still try. But honestly, that’s what scared me enough to come in. It’s like the warm part of me shut off. I’ll sit with my husband or put on my shows after work, and I know I should feel comfort or even a little happy, but there’s just… nothing there. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or anything, but it feels like the warm part of me kind of shut off. Like I’ll sit with my husband, or try to watch one of my shows after work, and I know I should feel comforted or even a little happy… but there’s just nothing there. I thought maybe I should talk to someone, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, like spending time with your husband or watching your favorite shows?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of exactly it. I’ll be sitting with my husband, or I’ll put on one of my shows after work, and I know those things usually make me feel settled or happy, even just a little… but lately it’s like I can’t really feel it. It’s just blank, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just during activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed exactly. I mean, I’m not trying to avoid the question, I just… most of the day, nearly every day sounds stronger than what I’ve noticed. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low for most of the day, even if it's not severe?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure I’d say most days, most of the day. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m fine, because I’m not, but “down or low” all day feels maybe stronger than what I can honestly say. It’s more like I feel flat or not quite myself. Is that an okay distinction?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on most days, even if not all the time, you feel down or low in your mood, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, maybe a little sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say most days. I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like I just don’t feel quite normal, if that makes sense—not necessarily sad all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: So, to make sure I understand, even if not all the time or very severe, would you say that on most days you feel at least a little down or low in your mood?\n\nPatient: I guess… if you put it that way, maybe a little, some days. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just during activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed exactly. I mean, I’m not trying to avoid the question, I just… most of the day, nearly every day sounds stronger than what I’ve noticed. I’ve felt off, and kind of flat, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m down all day. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low for most of the day, even if it's not severe?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure I’d say most days, most of the day. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m fine, because I’m not, but “down or low” all day feels maybe stronger than what I can honestly say. It’s more like I feel flat or not quite myself. Is that an okay distinction?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on most days, even if not all the time, you feel down or low in your mood, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, maybe a little sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say most days. I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like I just don’t feel quite normal, if that makes sense—not necessarily sad all the time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: So, to make sure I understand, even if not all the time or very severe, would you say that on most days you feel at least a little down or low in your mood?\n\nPatient: I guess… if you put it that way, maybe a little, some days. I’m just hesitant to say “most days” because I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I do feel kind of off or low at times, but I’m not sure I can say it’s most days. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: So, to clarify, would you say that more often than not, you feel a bit down or low in your mood, even if it's not all day or very strong?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I can honestly say more often than not. Maybe I have moments where I feel low, but it’s not like that most of the time. I just feel kind of not myself, if that makes sense. I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity that you used to really enjoy, even just a little, that you don’t enjoy anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess watching my shows after work, and just sitting with my husband. That used to feel comforting, like a nice part of the day. Now I’ll do it and I know I should feel relaxed or happy or something, but it’s kind of… blank. Like the warm part of me isn’t really there. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific activity that you used to enjoy, even just a little, where you now feel absolutely no interest or pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think it’s that.
